    | # ================= model.md: M5 =================
    | p = Path("docs/model.md"); t = p.read_text()
    | i = t.index("## Prediction ledger")
    | t = t[:i] + """## M5. Published trapped-state model (model F of Valse and Jannesari), evaluated for L1
    | 
    | **Use:** the predictions K3 will register (docs/claims.md). Added 2026-11-12 (decisions.md [CORR], fifth of that date). **Not this study's model and not evidence:** everything about the model is BELIEF, read from arXiv 2611.08902v1 §3 by the agent. It has predicted no run of this study yet, so it has no ledger row.
    | 
    | - Model: a single first-come-first-served server on which every turn misses; a session of T turns rejoins the queue after each turn; turn k costs c_k. With G_k = P(T ≥ k) and ω_k = c_k G_k / Σ c_j G_j, the root z > 1 of ρ Σ_k ω_k z^(−k) = 1 (ρ = load over all-miss capacity) gives: sessions completed over all-miss capacity D / λ_miss = ρ E[z^(−T)]; session backlog growth over excess load ρ (1 − E[z^(−T)]) / (ρ − 1); served-turn mix proportional to G_k z^(−k).
    | - Inputs for L1: T = 8 for every session; prompts 2176 + 256 (k − 1) tokens (FILE(configs/l1_base.yaml:9), FILE(PROTOCOL.md:54)); c_k taken as proportional to prompt length (an assumption; a cost with a fixed part per turn changes the values below by less than 0.02); ρ against the open-loop cold floor 1.681 req/s (REG-8 row of M2).
    | - Computed by `docs/paper/modelf_l1.py` (local, git-ignored; to move into `analysis/` with tests when step B is registered), DERIVED:
    |

    | | 2.0 | 1.19 | 0.89 | 1.56 | 0.984 | 1.016 (1.71 req/s) |
    | | 2.2 | 1.31 | 0.83 | 1.53 | 0.975 | 1.025 (1.72 req/s) |
    | | 2.4 | 1.43 | 0.78 | 1.51 | 0.967 | 1.034 (1.74 req/s) |
    | 
    | - These are long-run values. The model's authors report their own engine runs about 10 % from them in their judged window (BELIEF).
    | - Against runs on record (retrospective, not a test): the span completion rates of REG-17 (1.73–1.77 req/s at λ = 2.0) and REG-14 (1.73–1.81 at 2.2) lie 1–5 % above the model's request rates. Two explanations: the open-loop floor is a few percent low for closed-loop cells, or the runs are in a transient the model does not describe. The session completion rate and the session backlog slope have not been computed from any run.
    | - With a fixed turn count the model predicts fewer sessions than the all-miss capacity, the opposite of its authors' standard workload (geometric turn count), and matches their fixed-length test.

    | p = Path("docs/definitions.md"); t = p.read_text()
    | i = t.index("## Used configs that cannot be fixed (rule 5)")
    | t = t[:i] + """## Terms of the revised Stage 2 (added 2026-11-12, decisions.md [DECISION] of that date)
    | 
    | None has code yet; each gets its code when the run or analysis that uses it is registered.
    | 
    | ### cold floor (all-miss capacity)
    | - **Text:** the same quantity as μ_sat,off above: the completion rate of the saturated server with caching off. The preprints call it the all-miss capacity. The value on record for L1, 1.681 req/s, was measured with open-loop turns; a closed-loop value is not yet measured.
    | - **Code:** `gate.cold_floor`.
    | 
    | ### margin
    | - **Text:** the load divided by the cold floor. L1 at λ = 2.0: 2.0 / 1.681 = 1.19 (DERIVED).
    | - **Code:** none.
    | 
    | ### open-loop turns / closed-loop turns
    | - **Text:** open-loop: each turn is sent at its planned time, the gaps counted from the previous turn's planned time. Closed-loop: a later turn is released at the previous turn's end plus the planned gap (`workload.sessions.closed_loop: true`; FILE(configs/burst_l1pl350cl_r2.4_s1.yaml:6)). Latency is measured from the planned time in the first case and from the release time in the second (percent fast, above), so latency-based readouts are not comparable between the two.
    | - **Code:** `harness/workload.py`, `harness/loadgen.py`.
    | 
    | ### cache gain
    | - **Text:** the counter hit rate (`load._level` `"hit"`) of a setup's no-trigger control in its warm state.
    | - **Code:** `load._level`.
    | 
    | ### shared-only workload, matched cache gain
    | - **Text:** shared-only: every reusable token lies in one of a fixed set of prefixes shared across sessions, the set's total size does not change with load or backlog, and no request reuses tokens of its own session's earlier turns. Matched cache gain: two setups' cache gains agree within a tolerance registered beforehand. (`docs/claims.md`, K2.)
    | - **Code:** none; needs a harness change.
    | 
    | ### session cap, sessions in service
    | - **Text:** a session is in service from its first turn sent to its last reply received. The session cap is a limit on the number of sessions in service, held at the load generator; a session beyond it waits to start. Its value per seed is the highest number of sessions in service at once in the same seed's no-trigger control (decisions.md 2026-11-12 [DECISION], design choices).
    | - **Code:** none; needs a load-generator change.

    |     assert t.count(old) == n, (old[:70], t.count(old)); return t.replace(old, new)
    | def setline(t, prefix, new):
    |     L = t.split("\n"); idx = [i for i, l in enumerate(L) if l.startswith(prefix)]
    |     assert len(idx) == 1, (prefix, len(idx)); L[idx[0]] = new; return "\n".join(L)

    |     L = t.split("\n"); idx = [i for i, l in enumerate(L) if l.startswith(prefix)]
    |     assert len(idx) == 1, (prefix, len(idx)); del L[idx[0]]; return "\n".join(L)

    | # REG-19's rerun rule for one config, from the attempt records and the VM's checks.json files only. Returns 0 with
    | # RID set (one valid run exists: use it, launch nothing) or with LAUNCH set (the number of the attempt to launch);
    | # returns 2 with MSG set when the registration, or this decision, stops the queue.
    | #   - every launch is one attempt, whatever it left behind; at most MAX_ATTEMPTS attempts of a config;
    | #   - more run folders of the config on the VM than attempt records stop the queue (a launch outside these scripts);
    | #   - a run folder of the config on the Mac that the VM does not list stops the queue (a valid run already copied
    | #     must not be made a second time);
    | #   - a valid run is used only if it was made at the pin (env.json's git_commit);
    | #   - no rerun after a `no_errors` failure that holds a `client_error`, after `manipulation` (the burst did not fill
    | #     the pool) or after `power_limit` (a limit other than 350 W); one rerun after `gpu_steady` (heat), and a second
    | #     `gpu_steady` failure among ALL of REG-19's runs stops the queue;
    | #   - a run folder without checks.json (a lost run) is not rerun unless a decision allows it, recorded as
    | #     state/lost_ok_<run id>.txt. The record for seed 18's control of 2026-11-12 is committed with these scripts.
    | decide() {
    |   local cfg=$1 line parts f id n_valid=0 n_heat=0 norerun="" bad="" lost="" commit="" atts mine
    |   RID=""; LAUNCH=""; MSG=""; mine=()
    |   vm_state || { MSG="the VM's run folders could not be read"; return 2; }
    |   for line in $VMS; do
    |     parts=(${=line})
    |     for f in ${(s:,:)parts[3]}; do [[ $f == gpu_steady ]] && n_heat=$((n_heat + 1)); done   # over every REG-19 run
    |     [[ $parts[1] == *_$cfg ]] || continue
    |     mine+=($parts[1])
    |     case $parts[2] in
    |       valid) n_valid=$((n_valid + 1)); RID=$parts[1]; commit=$parts[4];;
    |       invalid)
    |         for f in ${(s:,:)parts[3]}; do
    |           case $f in
    |             no_errors/client_error|manipulation|power_limit) norerun="$f ($parts[1])";;
    |           esac
    |         done;;
    |       nochecks) [[ -f $ST/lost_ok_$parts[1].txt ]] || lost=$parts[1];;
    |       *) bad=$parts[1];;
    |     esac
    |   done
    |   atts=($ST/attempt_${cfg}_<->.txt(N))
    |   [[ -z $bad ]] || { RID=""; MSG="checks.json of $bad cannot be read"; return 2; }
    |   (( n_valid <= 1 )) || { RID=""; MSG="the VM holds $n_valid valid runs of $cfg: the owner decides"; return 2; }
    |   (( ${#mine} <= ${#atts} )) || { RID=""; MSG="the VM holds ${#mine} run folders of $cfg and only ${#atts} attempts are recorded in $ST"; return 2; }
    |   for id in $R/runs/*_${cfg}(N/:t); do
    |     [[ ${mine[(Ie)$id]} -gt 0 ]] || { RID=""; MSG="the Mac holds $id, which the VM does not list: nothing of $cfg is launched or used; the owner decides"; return 2; }
    |   done
    |   if (( n_valid == 1 )); then
    |     [[ $commit == $PIN ]] && return 0
    |     MSG="the valid run $RID of $cfg was made at commit '$commit', not at the pin: the owner decides"; RID=""; return 2
    |   fi
    |   [[ -z $norerun ]] || { MSG="REG-19 registers no rerun after $norerun: the owner decides"; return 2; }
    |   [[ -z $lost ]] || { MSG="$lost has no checks.json (lost or still running): a rerun needs its own decision, recorded as $ST/lost_ok_$lost.txt"; return 2; }
    |   (( n_heat < 2 )) || { MSG="second heat failure (gpu_steady) among REG-19's runs: REG-19 stops the queue; the owner decides"; return 2; }
    |   (( ${#atts} < MAX_ATTEMPTS )) || { MSG="the $MAX_ATTEMPTS attempts of $cfg are spent: operational stop, no table, the owner decides"; return 2; }
    |   LAUNCH=$((${#atts} + 1))
    |   [[ ! -e $ST/attempt_${cfg}_$LAUNCH.txt ]] || { MSG="attempt records of $cfg are not numbered 1..${#atts}"; LAUNCH=""; return 2; }
    |   return 0
    | }
    | 
    | # Is it this config's turn? Only the four configs of seeds 18 and 19; nothing of a seed that has a step record;
    | # seed 19 only after seed 18's record says 'continue': True; a burst run only after a valid control of its seed.
    | # launch.sh calls this before anything else, so a launch by hand cannot pass the futility stop or the order.
    | gate() {

    | # The checks in force (A14), applied read-only by analysis.recheck to REG-19's runs on the Mac, from main. $1 is the
    | # number of runs there must be. Only validity is kept: of the recheck's output, n_runs, n_changed and, for a changed
    | # run, its stored and recomputed `valid` and `failed`; the details of the checks are dropped before anything is
    | # written (they could hint at an outcome). Sets RK (the record) and RECHECK_COUNTS. Returns 0 when the count is $1 and
    | # nothing changed; 3 when not (an operational stop: the owner decides); 1 or 2 when the recheck could not be run.
    | RECHECK_FILTER='import json, sys
    | d = json.load(sys.stdin)
    | keep = ("run", "stored_valid", "now_valid", "stored_failed", "now_failed", "error")
    | print(json.dumps({"mac_head": sys.argv[1], "n_runs": d["n_runs"], "n_changed": d["n_changed"],
    |                   "changed": [{k: r[k] for k in keep if k in r} for r in d["changed"]]}, indent=1))'

    |   [[ ! -e $RK ]] || { MSG="$RK exists"; return 2; }
    |   raw=$(AFTERSHOCK_UNSEAL=REG-19 $PY -m analysis.recheck "$RECHECK_PAT" 2> $RKERR) || { MSG="analysis.recheck failed (see $RKERR)"; return 1; }
    |   print -r -- "$raw" | python3 -c "$RECHECK_FILTER" "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" > $RK || { MSG="the recheck's output cannot be read"; return 1; }
    |   RECHECK_COUNTS=$(python3 -c 'import json, sys; d = json.load(open(sys.argv[1])); print(d["n_runs"], d["n_changed"])' $RK) || { MSG="the recheck's record cannot be read"; return 1; }
    |   [[ $RECHECK_COUNTS == "$want 0" ]] && return 0
    |   MSG="the recheck gives '$RECHECK_COUNTS' (runs, changed) where '$want 0' is required (${RK:t}: validity fields only)"; return 3
    | }
